ABSTRACT
As Ho Chi Minh City solidifies its status as a premier economic and tourism hub, the demand for high-quality culinary experiences combined with convenient reservation processes has become increasingly urgent. However, localized overcrowding and a lack of transparent service information remain significant barriers for visitors. This thesis focuses on the research, design, and implementation of the website â€œFood Mapâ€, a comprehensive digital platform aimed at addressing the connectivity challenge between diners and food and beverage (F&B) establishments within Ho Chi Minh City.
Regarding technology, the system is constructed upon a modern architecture with MongoDB Geospatial serving as the core database. This optimizes geospatial storage and querying capabilities, enabling real-time localization and mapping of restaurants. Furthermore, to elevate the user experience, the application integrates an AI Chatbot powered by the Google Gemini API. Utilizing RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) techniques, the Chatbot functions as an intelligent culinary assistant capable of analyzing user preferences and requirements to extract data, thereby providing a curated list of the most suitable restaurant recommendations to facilitate user decision-making.
In terms of business operations, the project delivers a dual ecosystem optimized for both stakeholders. For users, the application offers an integrated utility process: allowing access to digital menus and enabling pre-ordering in parallel with remote table reservation. This helps users proactively manage their time and personalize their dining experience. For businesses, the system provides specialized management tools to monitor reservation status, thereby optimizing service capacity and minimizing the risk of unannounced cancellations (no-shows).
